     ax[0,0].set_ylim(-1, max_neurons_plot+1)\n   682→        ax[0,0].set_ylabel('Neural activity')\n   683→        ax[1,0].set_ylabel('VR environment')\n   684→        ax[2,0].set_ylabel('Distance to reward zone (cm)')\n   685→        ax[2,0].legend()\n   686→        ax[3,0].set_ylabel('Position (cm)')\n   687→        ax[4,0].set_ylabel('Speed (cm/s)')\n   688→        ax[5,0].set_ylabel('Lick')\n   689→        \n   690→        plt.tight_layout()\n   691→        plt.show()\n   692→        if figfile is not None:\n   693→            fig.savefig(figfile)\n   694→        \n   695→    return neural, input, output\n   696→\n   697→def survey(force=False, datadir=DATADIR, surveyfile=SURVEYFILE):\n   698→    if not force and os.path.exists(surveyfile):\n   699→        print(f\"Loading existing survey from {surveyfile}\")\n   700→        with open(surveyfile, 'rb') as f:\n   701→            return pickle.load(f)\n   702→    all_info = {}\n   703→    subjects = get_subjects(datadir=datadir)\n   704→    all_info['reward_zone_positions'] = {}\n   705→    fig,ax = plt.subplots(1,len(subjects), figsize=(2*len(subjects),20),sharex=True,sharey=True)\n   706→\n   707→    for i,subject in enumerate(subjects):\n   708→        sessions = get_sessions(subject, datadir=datadir)\n   709→        print(f\"Subject: {subject}, Sessions: {sessions}\")\n   710→        all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.zeros((0,2))\n   711→        for session in sessions:\n   712→            info = survey_session_data(subject, session, datadir=datadir)\n   713→            print(f\"Subject {subject}, Session {session}:\")\n   714→            for key, value in info.items():\n   715→                print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n   716→            for key,value in info.items():\n   717→                if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n   718→                    all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.concatenate((all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject], value), axis=0)\n   719→                elif key == 'neural_time_bin_size':\n   720→                    if 'neural_time_bin_size' not in all_info:\n   721→                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'] = [value,]\n   722→                    else:\n   723→                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'].append(value)\n   724→                elif not ('range' in key or 'unique' in key):\n   725→                    if 'range_'+key not in all_info:\n   726→                        all_info['range_'+key] = (value, value)\n   727→                    else:\n   728→                        all_info['range_'+key] = (min(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[0], value), max(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[1], value))\n   729→                elif key not in all_info:\n   730→                    all_info[key] = value\n   731→                elif 'range' in key:\n   732→                    all_info[key] = (min(all_info[key][0], value[0]), max(all_info[key][1], value[1]))\n   733→                elif 'unique' in key:\n   734→                    all_info[key] = np.unique(np.concatenate((all_info[key], value)))\n   735→                else:\n   736→                    raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected key in info: {key}\")\n   737→        # plot a horizontal line from reward_zone_positions[0] to reward_zone_positions[1] for each trial, with y value equal to the trial number\n   738→        reward_zone_positions = all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject]\n   739→        for trial in range(reward_zone_positions.shape[0]):\n   740→            ax[i].plot(reward_zone_positions[trial], [trial, trial], '-')\n   741→        ax[i].set_title(f\"Subject {subject}\")\n   742→\n   743→                    \n   744→    print(\"Summary across all sessions:\")\n   745→    for key, value in all_info.items():\n   746→        if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n   747→            continue\n   748→        elif 'range' in key:\n   749→            print(f\"  {key}: {value[0]} to {value[1]}\")\n   750→        elif 'unique' in key:\n   751→            print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n   752→        else:\n   753→            print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n   754→            \n   755→    ax[0].set_xlabel('Position (cm)')\n   756→    ax[0].set_ylabel('Trial')\n   757→    fig.tight_layout()\n   758→    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.png')\n   759→    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.pdf')\n   760→    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.svg')\n   761→    \n   762→    segment_results = segment_reward_positions(all_info['reward_zone_positions'])\n   763→    all_info['reward_zone_labels'] = {k: v['zone_labels'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n   764→    all_info['reward_zone_states'] = {k: v['states'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n   765→    all_info['reward_zone_dist'] = {k: v['zone_dist'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n   766→    \n   767→    with open(surveyfile, 'wb') as f:\n   768→        pickle.dump(all_info, f)\n   769→        \n   770→    return all_info\n   771→\n   772→def segment_reward_positions(reward_zone_positions):\n   773→    # assign each trial to one of the reward zones defined in reward_zone_dict\n   774→    # use viterbi algorithm with a high cost for switching to encourage stable assignments\n   775→    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n   776→    n_states = len(zone_names)\n   777→    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n   778→    pstay = .9\n   779→    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n   780→\n   781→    # transition log-probabilities\n   782→    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n   783→    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n   784→\n   785→    results = {}\n   786→\n   787→    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n   788→        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n   789→        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n   790→        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n   791→\n   792→        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n   793→        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n   794→        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n   795→        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n   796→        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n   797→                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n   798→        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n   799→        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n   800→\n   801→        # viterbi forward pass\n   802→        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n   803→        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n   804→        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n   805→        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n   806→            for k in range(n_states):\n   807→                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n   808→                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n   809→                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n   810→\n   811→        # backtrack\n   812→        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n   813→        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n   814→        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n   815→            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n   816→\n   817→        # convert state indices to zone names\n   818→        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n   819→\n   820→        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n   821→        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n   822→        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n   823→        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n   824→        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n   825→\n   826→        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n   827→\n   828→    return results\n   829→    \n   830→\n   831→def main():\n   832→\n   833→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Convert Sosa et al. 2024 NWB data to pickle format.')\n   834→ help='Directory containing NWB data files')\n   836→    parser.add_argument('--surveyfile', type=str, default=SURVEYFILE, help='Path to survey info pickle file (for saving/loading)')\n   837→    parser.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true', help='Plot visualizations of processing steps for up to 2 sessions')\n   838→    mode_group = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()\n   839→    mode_group.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='Process only 2 sessions for testing')\n   840→    mode_group.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True, = parser.parse_args()\n   843→\n   844→    # --sample implies showing at most 2 sessions\n   845→    max_sessions = 2 if args.sample else None\n   846→    show_processing = args.show_processing\n   847→    max_show_sessions = 2\n   848→\n   849→    info = survey(force=args.survey_only, datadir=args.datadir, surveyfile=args.surveyfile)\n   850→    if args.survey_only:\n   851→        print(f\"Survey complete. Results saved to {args.surveyfile}\")\n   852→        return\n   853→\n   854→    values, counts = np.unique(info['neural_time_bin_size'], return_counts=True)\n   855→    time_bin_size = find nans\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    # make nans 0\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n\n    # inversely weight nanned indices\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = .001\n    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter(a_nanless, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter(one, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    return a_nanless / one\n\n\ndef nansmooth_1d(a, sig, axis=-1, return_nans=False, **kwargs):\n    \"\"\"\n    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n    without propagating nans\n    :param a:\n    :param sig:\n    :return:\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n    (renamed: the TwoPUtils N-D version above already occupies the name `nansmooth`)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # find nans\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    # make nans 0\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n\n    # inversely weight nanned indices\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n\n    out = a_nanless / one\n    if return_nans:\n        out[nan_inds] = np.nan\n\n    return out\n\ndef dff(f,\n        trial_starts,\n        teleports,\n        f_neu=None,\n        neuropil_method=None,\n        baseline_method='maximin',\n        subtract_baseline=True,\n        neu_coef=0.7,\n        tau=0.7,\n        frame_rate=15,\n        n_planes=1,\n        deconvolve=False,\n        keep_teleports=False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate dFF for 1 channel\n\n    :param f: ROI fluorescence timeseries\n    :param trial_starts:\n    :param teleports:\n    :param f_neu: neuropil fluorescence timeseries\n    :param baseline_method:\n    :param subtract_baseline: True gives ∆F/F, False just normalizes F to the basline (divides only)\n    :param neu_coef:\n    :param deconvolve: whether to deconvolve the dff trace into \"events\" (psuedo spikes)\n    :return: dff and spks if deconvolve is True, otherwise just dff\n\n    `trial_starts` and `teleports` must be pandas Series: the keep_teleports branch calls\n    `.to_list()` on `teleports`, which a numpy array does not have. That is their code, unchanged.\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py::dff **\n    Keeps only the branches this dataset can reach (single-channel)\n    Dropped: bleedthrough_ts, neu_bleedthrough_ts, regress_ts, scrub_ts, neuropil_method='regress',\n    baseline_method='maxsmooth', subtract_baseline=False. \n    \"\"\"\n\n    print('baseline method =', baseline_method)\n\n    # Check frame rate if deconvolving\n    if deconvolve:\n        if (frame_rate/n_planes) > 16:\n            warnings.warn(\n                f\"Using frame rate per plane: {(frame_rate/n_planes)} -- is this correct?\")\n\n    f_ = np.zeros(f.shape) * np.nan\n\n    if f_neu is not None:\n        f_neu_ = np.zeros(f_neu.shape) * np.nan\n\n    if keep_teleports:\n        # if keeping the teleports, keep all the fluorescence from the start of imaging\n        # EXCEPT the teleport sample itself, as this position values might be interpolated between\n        # the end of the track and -50 (where the teleport jitter starts)\n        print(\"keeping teleports\")\n        start_inds = np.append(\n            trial_starts[0],\n            np.array(teleports[:-1] + 2)\n        ).tolist()\n        # ^ add 2 so with start-1 we will start 1 sample after the teleport\n        stop_inds = teleports.to_list()\n        # we will subtract 1 off this index below\n    else:\n        # if not keeping the teleports, keep only the fluorescence on each trial\n        start_inds = trial_starts.tolist()\n        stop_inds = teleports.tolist()\n\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n        f_[:, start - 1:stop -\n                 1] = nansmooth(f_[:, start-1:stop-1], [0, 15])\n\n            # minimum filter, taking min val over 20 sec\n            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(\n                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1) # was sp.ndimage.filters.minimum_filter1d\n            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(\n                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1)  # max filter with same window (dilation), was sp.ndimage.filters.maximum_filter1d\n\n    # to get deltaF/F: subtract baseline from initial signal, divide by abs(baseline)\n    # baseline can some times end up as negative due to regression\n    dff = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n    if subtract_baseline:\n        dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]\n                           ) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n\n    print('dff:', dff.shape, 'min ', np.nanmin(dff), 'max ', np.nanmax(dff))\n\n    # Smooth the deltaF/F transients by 2 time bins\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n        dff[:, start - 1:stop -\n            1] = nansmooth_1d(dff[:, start - 1:stop - 1], 2, axis=1)\n        # After smoothing: Get \"spikes\" with calcium kernel deconvolution from suite2p\n        if deconvolve:\n            spks[:, start-1:stop-1] = dcnv.oasis(\n                np.ascontiguousarray(dff[:, start-1:stop-1], dtype=np.float32),\n                2000, tau, (frame_rate/n_planes)) # this is float64 in the original code\n\n    if deconvolve:\n        return dff, spks\n    else:\n        return dff\n\n\ndef is_putative_interneuron(dff, speed, r_thresh=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find putative interneurons based on correlation of dF/F with speed\n    (under the hypothesis that fast-spiking interneurons in the pyramidal\n    layer are often highly correlated with running speed).\n\n    :param dff: (ncells, T) dF/F timeseries, NaN outside the analysed windows\n    :param speed: (T,) running speed in cm/s\n    :param r_thresh: threshold for speed correlation r; dayData sets int_thresh = 0.5, which is\n        the value the Methods quote (\"a Pearson correlation of >0.5\"). Note that the threshold\n        defaulted to 0.3 in the source function; the analyses always pass 0.5.\n    :return: is_int (array) with a Boolean for each ROI, where putative interneuron is True.\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/spatial.py::is_putative_interneuron **\n    Change: takes the dF/F and speed arrays as arguments instead of reading them off a TwoPUtils \n    `sess` object, which this conversion does not build.\n    \"\"\"\n    speed_corr = np.zeros((dff.shape[0],))\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\n\n    for c in range(dff.shape[0]):\n        speed_corr[c] = np.corrcoef(\n            dff[c, nanmask], speed[nanmask])[0, 1]\n\n    is_int = speed_corr > r_thresh\n\n    return is_int\n\n# end copy-paste\n# =================================================================================================\n\n\ndef get_subjects(datadir=DATADIR):\n    # ms\n\n        behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \n        istrial = behavior_data['trial number'].data[:] >= 0\n        # reward_amounts are all the same\n        reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:]\n        info['unique_reward_amounts'] = np.unique(reward_amounts)\n        info['nrewards'] = len(reward_amounts)\n        # negative if before the zone\n    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1] # positive if after the zone\n    return distance_to_reward_zone\n    \ndef convert_session_data(subject, session, info, trial_offset, time_bin_size=None, datadir=DATADIR, \n                         show_processing=False, max_neurons_plot=30, min_ntimepoints=50,\n                         figfile=None):\n\n    nwb_file_path = get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir)\n    io = pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(nwb_file_path, 'r')\n    nwbfile = io.read()\n\n    # parse neural activity data: the raw suite2p traces the paper computes its dF/F and\n    # deconvolved events from. Every step of dff is per-cell --\n        # its only cross-cell operation is a Gaussian with sigma 0 on the cell axis -- so this is\n        # numerically identical to subsetting afterwards, on a third of the memory.\n        fluorescence.append(fluorescence_curr[:,iscell])\n        neuropil.append(neuropil_curr[:,iscell])\n    T = fluorescence[0].shape[0]\n    # ms\n\n    behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n    \n    timestamps = behavior_data['trial number'].timestamps[:] \n    if T != len(timestamps):\n        print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n        T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n        fluorescence = [f[:T] for f in fluorescence]\n        neuropil = [f[:T] for f in neuropil]\n        timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n    \n    behavior_time_bin_size = np.median(np.diff(timestamps))*1000 # ms\n    \n    # trial number for each time point\n    trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], behavior_data['teleport'].data[:])\n    ntrials = len(trial_start_idx)\n    \n    # reward_amounts are all the same\n    reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:][:T]\n    assert np.allclose(reward_amounts,reward_amounts[0]), f\"Reward amounts are not all the same, unique values: {np.unique(reward_amounts)}\"\n    reward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:] # in seconds        \n    # find the closest timestamp to reward times, not careful for mismatches right now\n    reward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\n    err = timestamps[reward_time_indices] - reward_times\n    assert np.all(np.abs(err) <= time_bin_size/2), f\"Reward times do not align with neural time bins, max error: {np.max(np.abs(err))} s\"\n    isreward = np.zeros(T)\n    isreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n\n    # autoreward is all 0s, so not useful for modeling, but check that it is all zeros\n\n    # vr environment\n    vr_environment = behavior_data['environment'].data[:][:T] # one value per time point, does this change by session?\n    assert np.allclose(behavior_data['environment'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for environment do not match trial number timestamps\"\n\n    # current position in the VR environment\n    position = behavior_data['position'].data[:][:T]\n    assert np.allclose(behavior_data['position'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for position do not match trial number timestamps\"\n\n    # teleport -- not sure what to do with this, maybe should end the trial early??\n    #isteleport = behavior_data['teleport'].data[:][:T] == 1\n    #assert np.allclose(behavior_data['teleport'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for teleport do not match trial number timestamps\"\n\n    # speed\n    speed = behavior_data['speed'].data[:][:T] # cm / s\n    assert np.allclose(behavior_data['speed'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for speed do not match trial number timestamps\"\n\n    io.close()\n\n    assert len(timestamps) == T, f\"Number of timestamps ({len(timestamps)}) does not match number of neural time bins ({T})\"\n    if time_bin_size is not None:\n        assert np.isclose(neural_time_bin_size, time_bin_size, rtol=1e-2), f\"Neural time bin size ({neural_time_bin_size} ms) does not match specified time bin size ({time_bin_size} ms)\"\n        assert np.isclose(behavior_time_bin_size, time_bin_size, rtol=1e-2), f\"Behavior time bin size ({behavior_time_bin_size} ms) does not match specified time bin size ({time_bin_size} ms)\"\n    \n    # neural activity: the paper's deconvolved events, computed per plane because the\n    # deconvolution kernel depends on the per-plane sampling rate, frame_rate/nplanes.\n    #\n    # The released subject id m<N> is the paper's internal animal GCAMP<N>: methods.txt describes\n    # exactly the two groups this table holds -- \"except for mice m11-m14 on task days 1, 7, 8 and\n    # 14, and mice m15-m19 on day 1 and all switch days\" -- and names m17 and m18 as the two\n    # animals imaged in two planes, which are the two-plane sessions in this dataset. Session\n    # number is the experiment day: m11 starts at ses-03, and the paper says imaging for m11\n    # started on day 3.\n    keep_teleports = session in teleport_sessions.get(f'GCAMP{subject[1:]}', {}).get('exp_days', [])\n    spike_data = []\n    nint = 0\n    for fluorescence_curr, neuropil_curr in zip(fluorescence, neuropil):\n        # The trial indices are passed in shifted by +1: dff slices its windows as\n        # [start-1, stop-1), one sample earlier than the [trial_start, teleport) laps emitted here\n        # -- which is the window their own behavior.py:57 takes from the same two index arrays, so\n        # the two files already disagree by a sample upstream. Unshifted, the last sample of every\n        # lap comes out NaN (80 per session here) and one pre-lap sample gets a value instead;\n        # shifted, dff's windows land exactly on the emitted laps. The one cost is that on\n        # keep_teleports sessions the baseline window opens two samples after the teleport onset\n        # rather than their one.\n        dff_curr, events_curr = dff(fluorescence_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                                    pd.Series(trial_start_idx + 1), pd.Series(trial_end_idx + 1),\n                                    f_neu=neuropil_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                                    neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin',\n                                    subtract_baseline=True, neu_coef=0.7, tau=0.7,\n                                    frame_rate=frame_rate, n_planes=nplanes, deconvolve=True,\n                                    keep_teleports=keep_teleports)\n        # Drop the putative interneurons the paper excludes from further analysis.\n        isint = is_putative_interneuron(dff_curr, speed)\n        nint += int(np.sum(isint))\n        spike_data.append(events_curr[~isint].T)\n    spike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n    print(f\"Excluded {nint} putative interneurons of {nint + spike_data.shape[1]} cells \"\n          f\"({100*nint/(nint + spike_data.shape[1]):.2f}%)\")\n    # Samples between laps are NaN by construction (that is what restricting the baseline to the\n    # lap means); samples inside a lap must not be, since those are what the decoder sees.\n    on_trial = np.zeros(T, dtype=bool)\n    for start, stop in zip(trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx):\n        on_trial[start:stop] = True\n    assert not np.any(np.isnan(spike_data[on_trial])), \\\n        \"Deconvolved events are undefined somewhere inside a trial\"\n\n    neural = []\n    input = []\n    output = []\n    ninputs = len(input_names)\n    noutputs = len(output_names)\n    istrial = np.ones(ntrials,dtype=bool) # keep track of whether we have dropped a trial for having too few time points\n    min_distance_to_reward_zone = np.inf\n    max_distance_to_reward_zone = -np.inf\n    for trial in range(ntrials):\n        idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n        if idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n            print(f\"Skipping trial {trial} in subject {subject} session {session} because it has only {idx.sum()} < {min_ntimepoints} time points\")\n            istrial[trial] = False\n            continue\n\n        spike_data_curr = spike_data[idx]\n        timestamps_curr = timestamps[idx]\n        vr_environment_curr = vr_environment[idx].astype(int)\n        position_curr = position[idx]\n input_curr[1] = vr_environment_curr\n        \n        # trial number\n        input_curr[2] = trial\n        \n        # previous reward outcome\n        if trial == 0:\n            input_curr[3,:] = max_distance_to_reward_zone)\n\n        # histogram distance to reward zone into the specified bins\n        output_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1 # 0 = len(neural)\n        trials_plot = np.linspace(0, ntrials-1, min(ntrials, 5), dtype=int)\n        nbehplot = 5\n        if not np.isinf(max_neurons_plot):\n            sharey = 'row'\n                        sharex='col', sharey=sharey)\n        \n        distance_bin_centers = get_bin_centers(distance_to_reward_zone_bins, np.array([min_distance_to_reward_zone, triali].plot(timestamps_curr,  neural_curr[neuroni] / z + neuroni) # plot each neuron with an offset for visibility\n\n            ax[1,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,input_curr[1],'.-') # vr ax[2,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,distance_to_reward_zone,'.-',label='raw') # distance to reward zone\n ax[4,0].set_ylabel('Speed (cm/s)')\n        ax[5,0].set_ylabel('Lick')\n        \n        plt.tight_layout()\n        plt.show()\n        if figfile is not None:\n            fig.savefig(figfile)\n        \n    return neural, input, output\n\ndef survey(force=False, datadir=DATADIR, surveyfile=SURVEYFILE):\n    if not force and os.path.exists(surveyfile):\n        print(f\"Loading existing survey from {surveyfile}\")\n        with open(surveyfile, 'rb') as f:\n            return pickle.load(f)\n    all_info = {}\n    subjects = get_subjects(datadir=datadir)\n    all_info['reward_zone_positions'] = {}\n    fig,ax = plt.subplots(1,len(subjects), figsize=(2*len(subjects),20),sharex=True,sharey=True)\n\n    for i,subject in enumerate(subjects):\n        sessions = get_sessions(subject, datadir=datadir)\n        print(f\"Subject: {subject}, Sessions: {sessions}\")\n        all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.zeros((0,2))\n        for session in sessions:\n            info = survey_session_data(subject, session, datadir=datadir)\n            print(f\"Subject {subject}, Session {session}:\")\n            for key, value in info.items():\n                print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n            for key,value in info.items():\n                if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n                    all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.concatenate((all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject], value), axis=0)\n                elif key == 'neural_time_bin_size':\n                    if 'neural_time_bin_size' not in all_info:\n                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'] = [value,]\n                    else:\n                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'].append(value)\n                elif not ('range' in key or 'unique' in key):\n                    if 'range_'+key not in all_info:\n                        all_info['range_'+key] = (value, value)\n                    else:\n                        all_info['range_'+key] = (min(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[0], value), max(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[1], value))\n                elif key not in all_info:\n                    all_info[key] = value\n                elif 'range' in key:\n                    all_info[key] = (min(all_info[key][0], value[0]), max(all_info[key][1], value[1]))\n                elif 'unique' in key:\n                    all_info[key] = np.unique(np.concatenate((all_info[key], value)))\n                else:\n                    raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected key in info: {key}\")\n        # plot a horizontal line from reward_zone_positions[0] to reward_zone_positions[1] for each trial, with y value equal to the trial number\n        reward_zone_positions = all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject]\n        for trial in range(reward_zone_positions.shape[0]):\n            ax[i].plot(reward_zone_positions[trial], [trial, trial], '-')\n        ax[i].set_title(f\"Subject {subject}\")\n\n                    \n    print(\"Summary across all sessions:\")\n    for key, value in all_info.items():\n        if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n            continue\n        elif 'range' in key:\n            print(f\"  {key}: {value[0]} to {value[1]}\")\n        elif 'unique' in key:\n            print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n        else:\n            print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n            \n    ax[0].set_xlabel('Position (cm)')\n    ax[0].set_ylabel('Trial')\n    fig.tight_layout()\n    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.png')\n all_info['reward_zone_labels'] = {k: v['zone_labels'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n    all_info['reward_zone_states'] = {k: v['states'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n    all_info['reward_zone_dist'] = {k: v['zone_dist'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n    \n    with open(surveyfile, 'wb') as f:\n        pickle.dump(all_info, f)\n        \n    return all_info\n\ndef segment_reward_positions(reward_zone_positions):\n    # assign each trial to one of the reward zones defined in reward_zone_dict\n    # use viterbi algorithm with a high cost for switching to encourage stable assignments\n    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n    n_states = len(zone_names)\n    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n    pstay = .9\n    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n\n    # transition log-probabilities\n    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n\n    results = {}\n\n    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n\n        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n\n        # viterbi forward pass\n        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n            for k in range(n_states):\n                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n\n        # backtrack\n        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n\n        # convert state indices to zone names\n        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n\n        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n\n        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n\n    return results\n    \n\ndef main():\n\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Convert Sosa et al. 2024 NWB data to pickle format.')\n    parser.add_argument('outpicklefile', type=str, nargs='?', Switch days are handled by changing the zone label at trial 30 (the `SWITCH_TRIAL` constant).\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. For each time bin, the signed distance from the animal's binned position to the nearest edge of the reward zone is computed. Distance is 0 inside the zone, negative before, positive after. The position is first averaged over 8-frame bins before computing distance.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef reward_distance_cm(position_cm, zone_bounds):\n    start_cm, end_cm = zone_bounds\n    distance = np.zeros_like(position_cm, dtype=np.float32)\n    before = position_cm < start_cm\n    after = position_cm > end_cm\n    distance[before] = position_cm[before] - start_cm\n    distance[after] = position_cm[after] - end_cm\n    return distance\n```\n\niii. The computation matches the standard signed-distance-to-interval formula used in the reference.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 7 bins using explicit conditional logic matching the instruction bin edges: < -50, -50 to -10, -10 to 0, exactly 0, >0 to 10, 10 to 50, > 50.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_reward_distance(distance_cm):\n    out = np.empty(distance_cm.shape, dtype=np.uint8)\n    out[distance_cm < -50.0] = 0\n    out[(distance_cm >= -50.0) & (distance_cm < -10.0)] = 1\n    out[(distance_cm >= -10.0) & (distance_cm < 0.0)] = 2\n    out[distance_cm == 0.0] = 3\n    out[(distance_cm > 0.0) & (distance_cm <= 10.0)] = 4\n    out[(distance_cm > 10.0) & (distance_cm <= 50.0)] = 5\n    out[distance_cm > 50.0] = 6\n    return out\n```\n\niii. The bin edges match the instructions. The implementation uses explicit conditions rather than `np.digitize`.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Alignment is ensured by using the same frame indices and bin size.\n\nii.\n```python\npos_binned = bin_1d_mean(position[start:stop_trimmed], BIN_FRAMES)\ndistance_binned = reward_distance_cm(pos_binned.astype(np.float32), zone_bounds)\n```\n\niii. The position variable directly records the animal's VR position.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. Position is averaged over 8-frame bins and then clipped to [0, 450] cm before discretization.\n\nii.\n```python\npos_binned = bin_1d_mean(position[start:stop_trimmed], BIN_FRAMES)\npos_binned = np.clip(pos_binned, 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH_CM)\n```\n\niii. The clipping ensures all values fall within the track range.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 5 bins: < 90, 90 to 180, 180 to 270, 270 to 360, >= 360.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position_cm):\n    out = np.empty(position_cm.shape, dtype=np.uint8)\n    out[position_cm < 90.0] = 0\n    out[(position_cm >= 90.0) & (position_cm < 180.0)] = 1\n    out[(position_cm >= 180.0) & (position_cm < 270.0)] = 2\n    out[(position_cm >= 270.0) & (position_cm < 360.0)] = 3\n    out[position_cm >= 360.0] = 4\n    return out\n```\n\niii. Five equal 90 cm bins spanning the 450 cm track, matching the instructions.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Discretized into 5 bins: < 90, 90 to 180, 180 to 270, 270 to 360, >= 360.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position_cm):\n    out = np.empty(position_cm.shape, dtype=np.uint8)\n    out[position_cm < 90.0] = 0\n    out[(position_cm >= 90.0) & (position_cm < 180.0)] = 1\n    out[(position_cm >= 180.0) & (position_cm < 270.0)] = 2\n    out[(position_cm >= 270.0) & (position_cm < 360.0)] = 3\n    out[position_cm >= 360.0] = 4\n    return out\n```\n\niii. Five equal 90 cm bins spanning the 450 cm track, matching the instructions.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same 8-frame binning as neural data ensures alignment.\n\nii. `pos_binned = bin_1d_mean(position[start:stop_trimmed], BIN_FRAMES)`\n\niii. Same indexing scheme.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `lick` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nlick_counts = np.asarray(behavior[\"lick/data\"][:], dtype=np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The `lick` variable records lick events at each timepoint.\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick*?\n\ni. Lick values > 1.0 are capped at 1.0, then max-pooled over 8-frame bins, and binarized (> 0 becomes 1).\n\nii.\n```python\nlick_trial = lick_counts[start:stop_trimmed].copy()\nlick_trial[lick_trial > 1.0] = 1.0\nlick_binned = bin_1d_max(lick_trial, BIN_FRAMES)\nlick_binned = (lick_binned > 0).astype(np.uint8)\n```\n\niii. Max-pooling preserves lick events during binning. Values > 1.0 are clipped first (possibly related to the lick error threshold logic).\n\n## 9-c. How is `output` *Lick* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same 8-frame binning (using max instead of mean, but same frame boundaries) as neural data.\n\nii. Same `start:stop_trimmed` and `BIN_FRAMES`.\n\niii. Same binning scheme ensures alignment.\n\n## 10-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward zone location* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the NWB `identifier` field, which encodes the scene name (e.g., `Env1_LocationC_to_A`). The `parse_scene()` function extracts the reward zone label(s) and `expected_trial_labels()` assigns the correct zone to each trial, accounting for switch trials.\n\nii.\n```python\nscene = f[\"identifier\"][()].decode().split(\"/\")[-1]\nzone_labels, expected_envs = expected_trial_labels(scene, len(trial_segments))\n# ...\noutput_trial[4] = np.full(n_bins, ord(info[\"zone_label\"]) - ord(\"A\"), dtype=np.uint8)\n```\n\niii. The agent parsed the scene identifier to determine reward zone labels deterministically rather than using the `reward_zone` behavior time series or a Viterbi-based approach.\n\n## 10-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward zone location*?\n\ni. The scene name is parsed to extract zone labels before and after any switch. For switch sessions (e.g., `LocationC_to_A`), the zone changes at trial 30. The zone label (A/B/C) is converted to integer (0/1/2) using ASCII offset.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef expected_trial_labels(scene, n_trials):\n    info = parse_scene(scene)\n    if info[\"switch_trial\"] is None:\n        zones = [info[\"zone_before\"]] * n_trials\n    else:\n        pre_n = min(info[\"switch_trial\"], n_trials)\n        post_n = max(0, n_trials - pre_n)\n        zones = [info[\"zone_before\"]] * pre_n + [info[\"zone_after\"]] * post_n\n    return zones, envs\n```\n\niii. The agent uses a deterministic approach based on the session's scene identifier, with the switch point hardcoded at trial 30 based on the experimental protocol.\n\n## 11-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `Reward/timestamps` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_timestamps = np.asarray(behavior[\"Reward/timestamps\"][:], dtype=np.float64)\nreward_outcome = int(np.any(\n    (reward_timestamps >= frame_times[start]) & (reward_timestamps < frame_times[stop])\n))\n```\n\niii. Reward timestamps are checked against trial boundaries.\n\n## 11-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward outcome*?\n\ni. Binary: 1 if any reward timestamp falls within the trial's time window [start, stop), 0 otherwise. The value is constant for all time bins in the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_outcome = int(np.any(\n    (reward_timestamps >= frame_times[start]) & (reward_timestamps < frame_times[stop])\n))\n# ...\nnp.full(n_bins, info[\"reward_outcome\"], dtype=np.uint8)\n```\n\niii. Standard per-trial binary reward outcome.\n\n## 12. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several defensive measures: (1) NaN values in neural data are replaced with 0. (2) Environment and trial number signals are filtered for finite non-negative values with fallbacks to expected values from scene parsing. (3) Position is clipped to [0, 450]. (4) Lick values > 1 are clipped. (5) Sessions with < 2 trials are skipped. (6) Trials with lick sensor errors (>35% of frames with lick > 2) are excluded. (7) Speed NaN/inf replaced with 0.\n\nii.\n```python\nneural = np.nan_to_num(neural, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0).astype(np.float16)\npos_binned = np.clip(pos_binned, 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH_CM)\nspeed_binned = np.nan_to_num(speed_binned, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)\n```\n\niii. ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder. During testing, a reference copy of `/app/train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\nA successful conversion should:\n1. All required files must be created:\n  - `/app/convert_data.py`\n  - `/app/converted_data.pkl`\n2. Match the target data structure exactly\n3. Have consistent dimensions across trials/sessions\n4. Outputs must match expert-written conversion code in:\n  - Dataset size and distribution statistics\n  - Decoder accuracy\n7. was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter(one, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    return a_nanless / one\n\n\ndef nansmooth_1d(a, sig, axis=-1, return_nans=False, **kwargs):\n    \"\"\"\n    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n    without propagating nans\n    :param a:\n    :param sig:\n    :return:\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n    (renamed: the TwoPUtils N-D version above already occupies the name `nansmooth`)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # BIN_FRAMES)\n        pos_binned = bin_1d_mean(position[start:stop_trimmed], BIN_FRAMES)\n        speed_binned = bin_1d_mean(speed[start:stop_trimmed], BIN_FRAMES)\n\n        lick_trial = lick_counts[start:stop_trimmed].copy()\n        lick_trial[lick_trial > 1.0] = 1.0\n        lick_binned = bin_1d_max(lick_trial, BIN_FRAMES)\n        lick_binned = (lick_binned > 0).astype(np.uint8)\n\n        if neural is None or pos_binned is None or speed_binned is None or lick_binned is None:\n            continue\n\n        neural = np.nan_to_num(neural, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0).astype(np.float16)\n        pos_binned = np.clip(pos_binned, 0.0, TRACK_LENGTH_CM)\n        speed_binned = np.nan_to_num(speed_binned, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)\n\n        time_from_start = (\n            frame_times[start:stop_trimmed:BIN_FRAMES] - frame_times[start]\n        ).astype(np.float32)\n        prev_outcome = reward_outcomes[trial_idx - 1] if trial_idx > 0 else 0\n\n        input_trial = np.vstack(\n            [\n                time_from_start,\n                np.full(n_bins, info[\"environment\"], dtype=np.float32),\n                np.full(n_bins, info[\"trial_number\"], dtype=np.float32),\n                np.full(n_bins, prev_outcome, dtype=np.float32),\n            ]\n        ).astype(np.float32)\n\n        zone_bounds = REWARD_ZONES[info[\"zone_label\"]]\n        distance_binned = reward_distance_cm(pos_binned.astype(np.float32), zone_bounds)\n        output_trial = np.vstack(\n            [\n                discretize_reward_distance(distance_binned),\n                discretize_position(pos_binned),\n                discretize_speed(speed_binned),\n                lick_binned,\n                np.full(n_bins, ord(info[\"zone_label\"]) - ord(\"A\"), dtype=np.uint8),\n                np.full(n_bins, info[\"reward_outcome\"], dtype=np.uint8),\n            ]\n        ).astype(np.uint8)\n\n        neural_trials.append(neural)\n        input_trials.append(input_trial)\n        output_trials.append(output_trial)\n        zone_counter[info[\"zone_label\"]] += 1\n\n    plane_counts_curated = Counter(plane_idx_all[curated_cell_idx].tolist())\n    plane_counts_kept = Counter(plane_idx.tolist())\n    session_metadata = {\n        \"source_file\": str(path),\n        \"subject\": subject,\n        \"session_id\": session_id,\n        \"scene\": scene,\n        \"n_trials_total\": len(trial_segments),\n        \"n_trials_kept\": len(neural_trials),\n        \"n_trials_excluded_lick_error\": lick_error_trials,\n        \"n_curated_cells\": int(curated_cell_idx.size),\n        \"n_cells_kept\": int(keep_cells.sum()),\n        \"n_putative_interneurons_excluded\": int(is_interneuron.sum()),\n        \"putative_interneuron_fraction\": float(is_interneuron.mean()) if curated_cell_idx.size else 0.0,\n        \"putative_interneuron_speed_corr_range\": [\n            float(np.min(speed_corr)) if speed_corr.size else 0.0,\n            float(np.max(speed_corr)) if speed_corr.size else 0.0,\n        ],\n        \"environment_mismatch_trials\": env_mismatch_trials,\n        \"reward_zone_trial_counts_kept\": dict(zone_counter),\n        \"plane_counts_curated\": dict(plane_counts_curated),\n        \"plane_counts_kept\": dict(plane_counts_kept),\n    }\n\n    return {\n        \"subject\": subject,\n        \"session_id\": session_id,\n        \"scene\": scene,\n        \"neural\": neural_trials,\n        \"input\": input_trials,\n        \"output\": output_trials,\n        \"brain_region_idx\": np.zeros(int(keep_cells.sum()), dtype=np.int64),\n        \"metadata\": session_metadata,\n    }\n\n\ndef build_dataset():\n    paths = token count: 12440 Output: Total output lines: 189  # Overview  To dissociate reward from other sensory stimuli and observe how hippocampal activity related to rewards evolves with experience, we performed two-photon (2P) calcium imaging of CA1 neurons expressing GCaMP7f in head-fixed mice learning a virtual reality (VR) navigation task (Fig. 1a–c). VR provided tight control over sensory stimuli and the animal’s trajectory, allowing us to observe neuronal remapping in relation to distant rewards, a phenomenon potentially less visible in freely moving scenarios. Furthermore, the task included multiple updates to a hidden reward zone across two environments, allowing us to dissociate spatially driven and reward-driven remapping. Imaging began on day 1 of task acquisition, in which animals traversed a unidirectional 450 cm virtual linear track (for example, Environment 1 (ENV 1)) with a hidden 50 cm reward zone where sucrose water was delivered operantly for licking (Fig. 1d,e). Several cases are handled:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: If the number of neural time bins differs from behavior timestamps, data is cropped to the minimum of the two with a warning printed.\n- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 50 timepoints (`min_ntimepoints`) are skipped with a warning.\n- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nif T != len(timestamps):\n    print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n    T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n    spike_data = spike_data[:T]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n...\nif idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n    print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n    istrial[trial] = False\n    continue\n```\n\niii. all_info\n\ndef segment_reward_positions(reward_zone_positions):\n    # assign each trial to one of the reward zones defined in reward_zone_dict\n    # use viterbi algorithm with a high cost for switching to encourage stable assignments\n    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n    n_states = len(zone_names)\n    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n    pstay = .9\n    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n\n    # transition log-probabilities\n    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n\n    results = {}\n\n    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n\n        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n\n        # viterbi forward pass\n        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n            for k in range(n_states):\n                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n\n        # backtrack\n        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n\n        # convert state indices to zone names\n        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n\n        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n\n        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n\n    return results\n    \n\ndef main():\n\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Convert Sosa et al. 2024 NWB data to pickle format.')\n    parser.add_argument('outpicklefile', type=str, nargs='?', {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_19","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"rg -n \\\"30|switch\\\" /app/methods.txt /app/code -g '\"'!*.ipynb'\"' | sed -n '1,120p'\"","aggregated_output":"/app/methods.txt:3:To dissociate reward from other sensory stimuli and observe how hippocampal activity related to rewards evolves with experience, we performed two-photon (2P) calcium imaging of CA1 neurons expressing GCaMP7f in head-fixed mice learning a virtual reality (VR) navigation task (Fig. 1a–c). VR provided tight control over sensory stimuli and the animal’s trajectory, allowing us to observe neuronal remapping in relation to distant rewards, a phenomenon potentially less visible in freely moving scenarios. Furthermore, the task included multiple updates to a hidden reward zone across two environments, allowing us to dissociate spatially driven and reward-driven remapping. Imaging began on day 1 of task acquisition, in which animals traversed a unidirectional 450 cm virtual linear track (for example, Environment 1 (ENV 1)) with a hidden 50 cm reward zone where sucrose water was delivered operantly for licking (Fig. 1d,e). On day 3, the reward zone was moved within a ‘switch’ session to a new track location, signaled by automatic reward delivery on the first ten post-switch trials only if the mouse failed to lick in the new zone. On day 5, the reward zone was moved to a third location, then returned to its original location on day 7. On day 8, the reward switch coincided with the introduction of a novel environment (for example, ENV 2), in which the switch order was then reversed (Fig. 1e). On ‘stay’ days, the last reward location from the previous day was maintained. A separate ‘fixed-condition’ group of mice (n = 3) experienced only one reward location in a single environment (ENV 1) (Extended Data Fig. 1), to control for the effect of experience. To maintain engagement, the reward was randomly omitted on ~15% of trials. At the end of the track, mice passed through a variable length gray ‘teleport’ zone (~50 cm + temporal jitter up to 10 s; Methods) before starting the next lap.\n/app/methods.txt:9:Mice were pre-trained on a ‘running’ task on a 350 cm virtual linear track with random black and white checkerboard walls and a white floor to collect a cued reward. The reward location was marked by two large gray towers, initially positioned 50 cm down the track. The mouse was rewarded for licking within 25 cm of the towers, otherwise, an automatic reward was given at the towers. The towers then disappeared and quickly reappeared further down the track. Covering the distance to the current reward in under 20 s increased the inter-reward distance by 10 cm, but if the mouse took longer than 30 s, the distance decreased by 10 cm, with a maximum reward location of 300 cm. Upon consistent completion of 300 cm distance in under 20 s, the automatic reward was turned off, requiring the mice to lick to receive reward. At the end of the track, the mice passed through a black curtain and into a gray ‘teleport zone’ (50 cm long) that was equiluminant with the VR environment before re-entering the track from the beginning. Once mice were reliably licking and completing 200 laps of the pre-training track within a 30–40 min period, they were advanced to the main task below and imaging (mean ± s.d., 5.8 ± 1.9 days of running pre-training across 14 mice).\n/app/methods.txt:13:The main ‘switch’ task involved two virtual environments similar to those previously used to study hippocampal remapping, each with visually distinct features from the pre-training environment. Both environments consisted of a 450 cm linear track, with two colored towers (green in ENV 1, blue in ENV 2) and two patterned towers along the walls. Environments were further distinguished by the frequency of diagonal wall gratings (low, ENV 1; high, ENV 2) and color of the sky (dark gray, ENV 1; light gray, ENV 2). The reward zone was a ‘hidden’, unmarked 50 cm span at one of three possible locations along the track, each equidistantly spaced between the towers to control for proximity of each reward to visual landmarks: zone A, 80–130 cm; zone B, 200–250 cm; zone C, 320–370 cm. Only one reward zone was ever active at a time. Reward was randomly omitted on approximately 15% of trials, determined by a random number generator for each trial. Each lap terminated in a black curtain following by a ‘teleport period’ that began with an unchanging gray display for a randomly jittered amount of time (‘jitter period’) between 1 and 5 s (5–10 s on trials following reward omissions or trials in which the mouse did not lick in the reward zone), after which the gray display began to move again for 50 cm giving the appearance of a gray ‘tunnel,’ before the mouse re-entered the virtual environment at 0 cm.\n/app/methods.txt:15:Each mouse encountered a different starting reward zone and sequence of reward zone switches, counterbalanced across mice (n = 11 mice). Most mice began the task in ENV 1 (n = 9 mice; two mice (m17 and m18) began in ENV 2). An initial reward zone (for example, A as in Fig. 1e) was acquired for days 1–2 of the task. On day 3 (switch one), the zone was moved after 30 trials to one of the two other possible locations on the track (for example, B). On the first ten trials of any new condition, including the first day and each subsequent switch, the reward was automatically delivered at the end of the zone if the mouse had not yet licked within the zone, to signal reward availability. Otherwise, the reward was delivered at the first location within the zone where the mouse licked. After these ten trials, a reward was operantly delivered for licking within the zone, and we observed that the mice often started licking before ten trials elapsed (Extended Data Fig. 1a–c). The new reward zone was maintained on day 4. On day 5 (switch two), the zone was moved to the third possible reward zone (for example, C), maintained on day 6 and moved back to the original location on day 7 (for example, A). On day 8, the reward zone switch coincided with a switch into the novel environment, where the sequence of zone switches was then reversed on the same day-to-day schedule for a total of 14 days (Fig. 1 and Extended Data Fig. 1). Each switch occurred after 30 trials.\n/app/methods.txt:19:We targeted 80–100 trials per session with simultaneous 2P imaging (described below), with one imaging session per day (mean ± s.d., 80.5 ± 7.4 trials across 14 mice, all imaging days). The session was terminated early if the mouse ceased licking or running consistently and/or if the imaging time exceeded 50 min. Before the imaging session, mice were provided 30 ‘warm-up’ trials using the task and reward zone from the previous day to re-acclimate them to the VR setup (using the pre-training environment on day 1 of imaging). Following each daily imaging session, mice were given another ~100 training trials without imaging on the last reward zone seen in the imaging session until they acquired their daily water allotment.\n/app/methods.txt:23:We used a resonant-galvo scanning 2P microscope from Neurolabware with Scanbox software (https://scanbox.org, v.4.7) operated through MATLAB (Mathworks) to image the calcium activity of CA1 neurons. Excitation was achieved using 920 nm light from a tunable output femtosecond pulsed laser (Coherent Chameleon Discovery). Laser power modulation was accomplished with a Pockels cell (Conoptics). The typical excitation power, measured at the front face of the objective (Leica ×25, 1.0 NA, 2.6 mm working distance), ranged from 15–68 mW for mice m2–m7, 50–100 mW for m10 and m11, and 13–40 mW for m12–19. The task was imaged starting from day 1 for all mice except m11, for whom imaging started on day 3 owing to lower viral expression. For most animals and sessions, to minimize photodamage and photobleaching, the Pockels cell was used to reduce laser power to minimal levels between trials (during the teleport period), except for mice m11–m14 on task days 1, 7, 8 and 14, and mice m15–m19 on day 1 and all switch days, for which laser power was maintained and imaging continued throughout the teleport period. Photons were collected using Hamamatsu gated GAsP photomultiplier tubes (part H11706-401 for Neurolabware Microscopes). The imaging field of view (FOV) was collected at 1.0 magnification with unidirectional scanning at ~15.5 Hz, resulting in an approximately 0.7 × 0.7 mm FOV (512 × 796 pixels). An electrically tunable lens was used to simultaneously image deep and superficial CA1 in two mice (m17 and m18), in two planes separated by ~27 µm, with frames bidirectionally imaged at ~31 Hz interleaved in the scan for a sampling rate of ~15.5 Hz per plane. In all mice, the same FOV was acquired each session by aligning to a reference image from previous days before the start of data acquisition, with the aid of the ‘searchref’ plugin in Scanbox. This allowed us to track single cells across days.\n/app/methods.txt:37:The number of mice to include was determined by having coverage of every possible reward sequence permutation (six sequences) with at least one mouse in the switch task. In addition, our mouse sample sizes were similar to those reported in previous publications. Mice were randomly selected to experience the switch task (n = 11 mice) versus the ‘fixed-condition’ task in which the reward zone was not moved (n = 3).\n/app/methods.txt:45:The capacitive lick sensor allowed us to detect single licks. A very small number of trials with erroneous lick detection from damage to the circuit were removed from subsequent licking analysis by setting these values to NaN (~0.65% of all imaged trials, n = 81 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice). These trials were detected by >30% of the 0.0645 s imaging frame samples in the trial containing a cumulative lick count >2, as this would have produced a sustained lick rate ≥20 Hz. Remaining lick counts were converted to a binary vector and spatially binned at the same resolution as neural activity (10 cm bins), then divided by the time occupancy in each bin to yield a lick rate. There was no restriction to field boundaries, thus allowing cells to have multiple fields with a single identified peak.\n/app/methods.txt:73:We defined the cell remapping types shown in Fig. 2 and Extended Data Fig. 2 as follows: TR, significant SI before and after the switch, with spatial peaks (see ‘Spatial peak firing identification’) ≤50 cm from each other before versus after; disappearing, significant SI before but not after the switch, with a mean spatially binned dF/F after that is less than the 50th percentile of the per-trial mean dF/F before; appearing, significant SI after but not before, with a mean spatially binned dF/F after that is greater than 1 s.d. above the mean in trials before (appearing and disappearing cells that had sufficiently reliable activity despite their rate-remapping to be captured by the RR criteria below were removed from the appearing and disappearing groups); remap near reward, significant SI before and after, with spatial peaks ≤50 cm from the starts of both reward zones; and remap far from reward, significant SI before and after, not TR, with peaks >50 cm from the start of at least one reward zone.\n/app/methods.txt:79:For stringent statistics on putative RR remapping (Fig. 2c–i and Extended Data Fig. 2g–n), we restricted the set of included place cells to require significant SI in the pre-switch and post-switch trial sets in each session. We converted the position timeseries of the animal to periodic coordinates, setting 0 cm on the track to −π and 450 cm to π. Spatial peaks for each cell were re-computed using binned dF/F at a periodic bin size of 2π / 45 (corresponding to ~10 cm). Putative TR cells were removed by excluding cells with spatial peaks within ~0.698 radians (50 cm) of each other before versus after the switch. The periodic coordinates were then circularly rotated to align the start of each reward zone at 0, to measure the signed circular distance of spatial peaks relative to the start of each reward zone. For scatter plots of spatial peaks in Fig. 2 and Extended Data Fig. 2, points are jittered by a random amount between −π / 100 and +π / 100 for visualization only. We measured the circularly wrapped difference between relative peaks after minus before the switch, creating a distribution that can be thought of as orthogonal to the unity line in Fig. 2d,f. We compared this distribution to a ‘random-remapping’ shuffle, generated by maintaining the pre-switch peak of each cell and circularly permuting its post-switch firing 1,000 times by a random offset of 0–44 bins. To define a candidate range of RR remapping variability, we computed the circular difference between the maximum and minimum bin of the distribution of true differences between RR peaks that exceeded the upper 95% of the shuffle, divided by two and averaged across switches. In an initial cohort of n = 7 mice, this produced a mean range of ±0.656 radians (~46.9 cm) around 0, which is captured by a 50 cm threshold given our 10 cm bin size. This threshold was subsequently used to identify candidate RR remapping cells (see below).\n/app/methods.txt:83:As we observed variable remapping dynamics over trials after a reward switch (Fig. 2a,b and Extended Data Fig. 2a), which could affect SI scores, we relaxed the SI criterion for RR cells such that they were required to have significant SI either before or after the switch but not necessarily both. We next implemented two criteria to identify a robust subpopulation of RR cells. First, we identified place cells with peak spatial firing relative to the reward zone start within ~0.698 radians (50 cm) of each other before versus after a reward switch (within the orange candidate zone highlighted in Fig. 2d,f), allowing for some variability in RR precision. These cells could thus include ‘remap near reward’ and ‘remap far from reward’ cells (see ‘Remapping category definitions’). Second, to reduce the influence of noise in spatial peak detection and account for the shape of the firing fields, we cross-correlated the cells’ trial-averaged, spatially binned dF/F aligned to the reward zone pre-switch versus post-switch. We predicted that RR cells should have a peak cross-correlation close to zero on this reward-centered axis. We calculated a shuffle for the cross-correlation by circularly permuting the activity in each post-switch trial by a random offset between 1 and 45 bins 500 times, requiring that the real cross-correlogram had a peak exceeding the upper 97.5% confidence interval of the shuffle and that the offset of this peak was within five bins (~50 cm) of zero. This approach provides a metric for how stable each cell’s firing is relative to reward, identifying a subpopulation of cells whose fields were maximally Pearson-correlated in periodic coordinates relative to reward, in contrast to the TR cells, which were maximally correlated relative to the original linear track coordinates (Extended Data Fig. 2o–q). Place cells that passed both criteria were defined as RR.\n/app/methods.txt:87:We measured the distance run in the teleport zone at the end of each trial by integrating the animal’s speed from the first frame in the teleport period to the last frame before re-entry to the start of the track. We then found the first five-trial window with at least three trials above this threshold and took the first active trial in that window as the formation lap31. To compute the field’s center of mass on each lap with maximum spatial resolution, we used the raw deconvolved activity and position within the bounds of the trial-averaged field. We then subtracted the center of mass on the formation lap from the center of mass of the field’s mean activity in the last 30 trials of the session to quantify the degree of shift (negative values indicate a backwards shift).\n/app/methods.txt:104:We implemented a previously developed decoder that uses circular–linear regression38 to predict the animal’s position in circular, RR coordinates from the deconvolved calcium event timeseries (at speeds of >2 cm s−1) of RR, TR or non-RR remapping neurons. The model ‘decode score’ at each timepoint t is given by \\cos ({{y}_{t}}-\\hat{{y}}_{t}), where is the animal’s true RR position and \\hat{{y}}_{t} is the predicted RR position. A score of one indicates perfect prediction and zero indicates random prediction. We performed tenfold cross-validation in which we trained the decoder on 90% of the data and tested it on a held-out 10%, either training and testing within the trials before the switch or training on one set (before) and testing on the other set (after). Figure 3a shows decoder performance from a single cross-validation fold, considering all data from an example session. To quantify decoding accuracy compared to chance, we next downsampled the data to match occupancy at each RR position bin (bin size 2π / 45, ~10 cm), either within trials before the switch or in the last 30 trials after the switch (thereby matching trial numbers before and after). We then repeated model training and testing with 100 shuffled datasets for each session and cell subpopulation. In each shuffle, the timeseries of each cell was independently circularly shifted by a uniform random amount up to the number of samples in the session minus one. In Fig. 3b, model performance is reported as a mean decode score over time samples and cross-validation folds compared to the mean of the shuffles for each session. We sorted neurons by their peak firing positions from activity averaged over the odd trials before the switch. This sort was cross-validated by plotting the trial-averaged activity of the even trials before the switch (for example, left-hand columns of Fig. 4a, normalized to the mean activity of each cell within a session), then applying this sort to the trials after the switch. The sequence positions before versus after were then taken as the peak firing positions of the trial-averaged ‘even-before’ trials and the trial-averaged ‘after’ trials. Preservation of sequence order was quantified as the circular–circular correlation coefficient between the sequence positions before versus after. Although a P value can be directly computed for this correlation coefficient, its significance was further validated by randomly permuting the cell identities of the neurons after the switch and re-computing the correlation coefficient 1,000 times to obtain a null distribution. These models apply warping functions of increasing nonlinear complexity to stretch and compress the data on each trial for maximal alignment. We included both rewarded and omission trials in the fitting procedure to find the best alignment across them. Model fit was assessed using the mean squared error between the time-warped speed profile of each trial and the mean across time-warped trials (Extended Data Fig. 8a). The piecewise three knots model most often produced the best fit (lowest mean squared error) across sessions (Extended Data Fig. 8c). We therefore re-fit all sessions with piecewise three knots to ensure that model selection could not influence the results.
